Circular Motion (Kroženje)\n\nThis section covers the principles of circular motion, including uniform and non-uniform (accelerated) motion.\n\n### Key Concepts and Formulas\n\n| Formula | Description | Application \u0026 Derivation |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **Linear Velocity (Obodna hitrost)** | | |\n| $v \u003d \\frac{\\Delta s}{\\Delta t}$ | Linear velocity ($v$) is the rate of change of the arc length ($s$) over time ($t$). Units: m/s. | Used to find the speed of an object moving along the circumference. |\n| **Angular Velocity (Kotna hitrost)** | | |\n| $\\omega \u003d \\frac{\\Delta \\phi}{\\Delta t}$ | Angular velocity ($\\omega$) is the rate of change of the angle ($\\phi$) over time ($t$). Units: rad/s or s⁻¹. | Describes how fast an object is rotating. |\n| **Period and Frequency (Obhodni čas in Frekvenca)** | | |\n| $t_0 \u003d \\frac{1}{\\nu}$ | The period ($t_0$) is the time for one full revolution. Frequency ($\\nu$) is the number of revolutions per second. Unit of frequency: Hz (s⁻¹). | These are fundamental properties of periodic motion. |\n| **Relationship between Velocities** | | |\n| $v \u003d \\omega \\cdot r$ | Linear velocity is the product of angular velocity and the radius ($r$) of the circle. | Links the rotational motion ($\\omega$) to the linear motion ($v$) along the edge. |\n| $\\omega \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot \\nu \u003d \\frac{2\\pi}{t_0}$ | Angular velocity expressed in terms of frequency or period. One full circle is $2\\pi$ radians. | Essential for converting between different measures of rotational speed. |\n| **Accelerations in Circular Motion** | | |\n| $\\vec{a}_r \u003d \\frac{v^2}{r} \u003d \\omega^2 \\cdot r$ | **Radial (Centripetal) Acceleration ($a_r$)**. Always directed towards the center of the circle. It\u0027s responsible for changing the *direction* of the velocity vector. | This acceleration is necessary to keep an object in circular motion. |\n| $\\vec{a}_t \u003d \\alpha \\cdot r$ | **Tangential Acceleration ($a_t$)**. Directed along the tangent to the circle. It\u0027s responsible for changing the *magnitude* of the velocity (the speed). | This is only present in non-uniform (accelerated) circular motion. |\n| $\\vec{a}_s \u003d \\sqrt{a_r^2 + a_t^2}$ | **Total Acceleration ($a_s$)**. The vector sum of the radial and tangential accelerations. | Gives the overall acceleration of an object in non-uniform circular motion. |\n| **Angular Acceleration (Kotni pospešek)** | | |\n| $\\alpha \u003d \\frac{\\Delta \\omega}{\\Delta t}$ | Angular acceleration ($\\alpha$) is the rate of change of angular velocity. Units: rad/s² or s⁻². | Describes how quickly the rate of rotation is changing. |\n| **Rotational Kinematics** | | |\n| $\\phi(t) \u003d \\phi_0 + \\omega_0 t + \\frac{1}{2}\\alpha t^2$ | Angular position ($\\phi$) as a function of time for constant angular acceleration. | The rotational equivalent of the linear motion equation $x(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0 t + \\frac{1}{2}at^2$. |\n| $N \u003d \\frac{\\phi}{2\\pi}$ | The number of revolutions ($N$) is the total angle traversed divided by the angle of one revolution ($2\\pi$). | Used to calculate the total number of turns an object makes. |\n\n---\n\n### Solved Problems\n\n#### Problem 1: Uniform Circular Motion\n\n- **Given:** $r \u003d 50 \\, \\text{cm}$, $v \u003d 8 \\, \\text{cm/s}$\n- **Find:** \n    - a) Distance ($x$) traveled in $t \u003d 60 \\, \\text{s}$.\n    - b) Time ($t_{100}$) for 100 revolutions.\n\n**a) Distance in 60 seconds:**\n- **Formula:** $v \u003d \\frac{x}{t} \\implies x \u003d v \\cdot t$\n- **Solution:**\n$$\nx \u003d (8 \\, \\text{cm/s}) \\cdot (60 \\, \\text{s}) \u003d 480 \\, \\text{cm}\n$$\n\n**b) Time for 100 revolutions:**\n1.  **Find the circumference (distance of one revolution):**\n    - **Formula:** $l \u003d 2\\pi r$\n    - **Solution:** $l \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot (50 \\, \\text{cm}) \u003d 100\\pi \\, \\text{cm}$\n2.  **Find the total distance for 100 revolutions:**\n    - **Solution:** $L_{100} \u003d 100 \\cdot l \u003d 100 \\cdot (100\\pi) \u003d 10000\\pi \\, \\text{cm}$\n3.  **Find the time required:**\n    - **Formula:** $t \u003d \\frac{L}{v}$\n    - **Solution:**\n$$\nt_{100} \u003d \\frac{10000\\pi \\, \\text{cm}}{8 \\, \\text{cm/s}} \u003d 1250\\pi \\, \\text{s} \\approx 3927 \\, \\text{s} \\approx 65.5 \\, \\text{min}\n$$\n\n---\n\n#### Problem 2: Revolutions and Velocity\n\n- **Given:** $t\u003d2\\,s$, $N \u003d 1.5$ revolutions, $r\u003d20\\,cm$\n- **Find:**\n    - a) Angular velocity ($\\omega$) and linear velocity ($v$).\n    - b) Arc length ($l$) traveled in $t \u003d 10 \\, \\text{s}$.\n\n**a) Angular and Linear Velocity:**\n1.  **Find the frequency ($\\nu$):**\n    - **Formula:** $\\nu \u003d \\frac{N}{\\Delta t}$\n    - **Solution:** $\\nu \u003d \\frac{1.5}{2 \\, \\text{s}} \u003d 0.75 \\, \\text{Hz}$\n2.  **Find the angular velocity ($\\omega$):**\n    - **Formula:** $\\omega \u003d 2\\pi \\nu$\n    - **Solution:** $\\omega \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot (0.75 \\, \\text{s}^{-1}) \u003d 1.5\\pi \\, \\text{s}^{-1}$\n3.  **Find the linear velocity ($v$):**\n    - **Formula:** $v \u003d \\omega \\cdot r$\n    - **Solution:** $v \u003d (1.5\\pi \\, \\text{s}^{-1}) \\cdot (20 \\, \\text{cm}) \u003d 30\\pi \\, \\text{cm/s}$\n\n**b) Arc length in 10 seconds:**\n- **Formula:** $l \u003d v \\cdot t$ (since velocity is constant)\n- **Solution:**\n$$\nl \u003d (30\\pi \\, \\text{cm/s}) \\cdot (10 \\, \\text{s}) \u003d 300\\pi \\, \\text{cm}\n$$\n\n---\n\n#### Problem 3: Radial Acceleration\n\n- **Given:** $r \u003d 2 \\, \\text{cm}$, $\\nu \u003d 2.78 \\cdot 10^{-4} \\, \\text{s}^{-1}$\n- **Find:** Radial acceleration ($a_r$).\n\n1.  **Find the linear velocity ($v$):**\n    - We first need $\\omega$: $\\omega \u003d 2\\pi \\nu$\n    - Then use $v \u003d \\omega \\cdot r \u003d (2\\pi \\nu) \\cdot r$\n    - **Solution:** $v \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot (2.78 \\cdot 10^{-4} \\, \\text{s}^{-1}) \\cdot (2 \\, \\text{cm}) \\approx 3.5 \\cdot 10^{-3} \\, \\text{cm/s}$\n2.  **Find the radial acceleration ($a_r$):**\n    - **Formula:** $a_r \u003d \\frac{v^2}{r}$\n    - **Solution:**\n$$\na_r \u003d \\frac{(3.5 \\cdot 10^{-3} \\, \\text{cm/s})^2}{2 \\, \\text{cm}} \\approx 6.1 \\cdot 10^{-6} \\, \\text{cm/s}^2\n$$\n\n---\n#### Problem 6: Non-Uniform Motion \u0026 Angular Acceleration\n\n- **Given:** $\\Delta t \u003d 50 \\, \\text{s}$, $\\nu_1 \u003d 300 \\, \\text{min}^{-1}$, $\\nu_2 \u003d 600 \\, \\text{min}^{-1}$\n- **Find:** Angular acceleration ($\\alpha$).\n\n1.  **Convert frequencies to standard units (Hz or s⁻¹):**\n    - $\\nu_1 \u003d \\frac{300}{60} \u003d 5 \\, \\text{s}^{-1}$\n    - $\\nu_2 \u003d \\frac{600}{60} \u003d 10 \\, \\text{s}^{-1}$\n2.  **Calculate initial and final angular velocities ($\\omega_1, \\omega_2$):**\n    - **Formula:** $\\omega \u003d 2\\pi \\nu$\n    - **Solution:**\n        - $\\omega_1 \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot 5 \u003d 10\\pi \\, \\text{rad/s}$\n        - $\\omega_2 \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot 10 \u003d 20\\pi \\, \\text{rad/s}$\n3.  **Calculate the change in angular velocity ($\\Delta \\omega$):**\n    - **Solution:** $\\Delta \\omega \u003d \\omega_2 - \\omega_1 \u003d 20\\pi - 10\\pi \u003d 10\\pi \\, \\text{rad/s} \\approx 31.4 \\, \\text{rad/s}$\n4.  **Calculate angular acceleration ($\\alpha$):**\n    - **Formula:** $\\alpha \u003d \\frac{\\Delta \\omega}{\\Delta t}$\n    - **Solution:**\n$$\n\\alpha \u003d \\frac{31.4 \\, \\text{rad/s}}{50 \\, \\text{s}} \\approx 0.628 \\, \\text{rad/s}^2\n$$\n\n---\n#### Problem 8: Deceleration and Revolutions\n\n- **Given:** $\\nu_1 \u003d 600 \\, \\text{min}^{-1}$, $\\nu_2 \u003d 0 \\, \\text{Hz}$ (comes to a stop), $\\Delta t \u003d 30 \\, \\text{s}$\n- **Find:** Number of revolutions ($N$) until it stops.\n\n1. **Calculate initial angular velocity ($\\omega_1$):**\n   - $\\nu_1 \u003d \\frac{600}{60} \u003d 10 \\, \\text{s}^{-1}$\n   - $\\omega_1 \u003d 2\\pi \\nu_1 \u003d 2\\pi \\cdot 10 \u003d 20\\pi \\, \\text{rad/s}$\n2. **Calculate angular acceleration ($\\alpha$):**\n   - $\\Delta \\omega \u003d \\omega_2 - \\omega_1 \u003d 0 - 20\\pi \u003d -20\\pi \\, \\text{rad/s}$\n   - **Formula:** $\\alpha \u003d \\frac{\\Delta \\omega}{\\Delta t}$\n   - **Solution:** $\\alpha \u003d \\frac{-20\\pi \\, \\text{rad/s}}{30 \\, \\text{s}} \u003d -\\frac{2}{3}\\pi \\, \\text{s}^{-2}$\n3. **Calculate total angle traversed ($\\phi$):**\n   - **Formula:** $\\phi(t) \u003d \\omega_1 t + \\frac{1}{2}\\alpha t^2$\n   - **Solution:**\n$$\n\\phi \u003d (20\\pi)(30) + \\frac{1}{2}(-\\frac{2}{3}\\pi)(30)^2 \u003d 600\\pi - \\frac{1}{3}\\pi(900) \u003d 600\\pi - 300\\pi \u003d 300\\pi \\, \\text{rad}\n$$\n4. **Calculate the number of revolutions ($N$):**\n   - **Formula:** $N \u003d \\frac{\\phi}{2\\pi}$\n   - **Solution:**\n$$\nN \u003d \\frac{300\\pi}{2\\pi} \u003d 150 \\text{ revolutions (obratov)}\n$$\n\n```",

      "text": "Excellent questions! These get to the very heart of the relationship between physics and calculus, and the fundamental concepts of rotational motion. Let\u0027s break them down.\n\n---\n\n### 1. Kinematics, Integration, and Jerk\n\nYou are absolutely correct! The kinematic equation:\n$$\nx(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0 t + \\frac{1}{2}at^2\n$$\n...is a direct result of **integration**, based on the assumption that acceleration ($a$) is **constant**.\n\nLet\u0027s build it from the ground up. The relationship between position, velocity, and acceleration is defined by calculus:\n\n-   **Velocity ($v$)** is the rate of change (derivative) of position ($x$). $v \u003d \\frac{dx}{dt}$\n-   **Acceleration ($a$)** is the rate of change (derivative) of velocity ($v$). $a \u003d \\frac{dv}{dt}$\n\nThis means we can go in reverse using integration:\n\n1.  **From Acceleration to Velocity:**\n    If we assume acceleration is a constant value, $a_0$, we can find velocity by integrating it with respect to time:\n    $$\n    v(t) \u003d \\int a_0 \\, dt \u003d a_0t + C\n    $$\n    The constant of integration, $C$, represents the velocity at $t\u003d0$, which we call the **initial velocity, $v_0$**.\n    So, we get the first kinematic equation:\n    $$\n    v(t) \u003d v_0 + a_0t\n    $$\n\n2.  **From Velocity to Position:**\n    Now we integrate the velocity function we just found:\n    $$\n    x(t) \u003d \\int v(t) \\, dt \u003d \\int (v_0 + a_0t) \\, dt\n    $$\n    $$\n    x(t) \u003d v_0t + \\frac{1}{2}a_0t^2 + C\n    $$\n    This new constant of integration, $C$, is the position at $t\u003d0$, which we call the **initial position, $x_0$**.\n    This gives us the familiar full equation:\n    $$\n    x(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0t + \\frac{1}{2}a_0t^2\n    $$\n\n#### Expanding to Jerk and Beyond\n\n**Yes, this pattern absolutely continues!** You\u0027re thinking like a physicist.\n\n**Jerk ($j$)** is the rate of change of acceleration ($j \u003d \\frac{da}{dt}$). If we assume **jerk is constant ($j_0$)**, we can integrate three times:\n\n1.  $a(t) \u003d \\int j_0 \\, dt \u003d j_0t + a_0$\n2.  $v(t) \u003d \\int (j_0t + a_0) \\, dt \u003d \\frac{1}{2}j_0t^2 + a_0t + v_0$\n3.  $x(t) \u003d \\int (\\frac{1}{2}j_0t^2 + a_0t + v_0) \\, dt \u003d \\frac{1}{6}j_0t^3 + \\frac{1}{2}a_0t^2 + v_0t + x_0$\n\nNotice the pattern in the coefficients: $1, \\frac{1}{2}, \\frac{1}{6}$. This corresponds to $\\frac{1}{1!}, \\frac{1}{2!}, \\frac{1}{3!}$. The pattern continues for even higher derivatives like Snap, Crackle, and Pop.\n\nHere is a summary table you can add to your notes:\n\n| Derivative | Name | Relationship | Integral for Constant Value |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| $\\frac{dx}{dt}$ | Velocity ($v$) | $\\int v(t) dt \u003d x(t)$ | $x(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0t$ |\n| $\\frac{d^2x}{dt^2}$ | Acceleration ($a$) | $\\int a(t) dt \u003d v(t)$ | $x(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0t + \\frac{1}{2}a_0t^2$ |\n| $\\frac{d^3x}{dt^3}$ | Jerk ($j$) | $\\int j(t) dt \u003d a(t)$ | $x(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0t + \\frac{1}{2}a_0t^2 + \\frac{1}{6}j_0t^3$ |\n\n---\n\n### 2. Angular Velocity ($\\omega$) vs. Frequency ($\\nu$)\n\nThis is an excellent and very common point of confusion. The key is to think about what each term is actually measuring. The factor of $2\\pi$ is simply a conversion factor between \"counts\" and \"angle\".\n\nLet\u0027s use an analogy of a carousel.\n\n#### Frequency ($\\nu$): \"The Counter\"\n-   **What it asks:** \"How many full laps does the carousel make in one second?\"\n-   **What it measures:** A **count** of complete cycles or revolutions.\n-   **Units:** Revolutions per second, or **Hertz (Hz)**.\n-   **Analogy:** You are standing on the ground watching the carousel. You use a stopwatch and a clicker. In 10 seconds, you click 5 times. Your frequency is $\\nu \u003d \\frac{5 \\text{ laps}}{10 \\text{ s}} \u003d 0.5$ laps/second \u003d **0.5 Hz**.\n\n#### Angular Velocity ($\\omega$): \"The Rider\"\n-   **What it asks:** \"How much *angle* are you sweeping through each second?\"\n-   **What it measures:** The **rate of rotation** or the speed of turning.\n-   **Units:** Angle per second. The standard unit for angle in physics is the **radian**, so it\u0027s **radians per second (rad/s)**.\n-   **Analogy:** You are now sitting on a horse on the carousel. You aren\u0027t counting laps; you are *feeling* the continuous turn. Your world is rotating at a certain speed. This turning speed is your angular velocity.\n\n#### The Bridge: Why $2\\pi$?\n\nThe connection is a simple geometric fact:\n\u003e **One full revolution (one lap) is equal to $2\\pi$ radians.** (Just like one lap is 360°).\n\nSo, $2\\pi$ is the conversion factor that translates the \"lap count\" into the \"total angle swept\".\n\nLet\u0027s build the formula logically:\n- If your frequency is $\\nu$ (laps per second).\n- And each lap contains $2\\pi$ radians of angle.\n- Then the total angle you sweep through per second must be:\n$$\n(\\text{laps per second}) \\times (\\text{radians per lap}) \u003d (\\nu) \\times (2\\pi)\n$$\nAnd the \"angle swept per second\" is the very definition of angular velocity, $\\omega$.\nTherefore:\n$$\n\\omega \u003d 2\\pi\\nu\n$$\n\n**In short:**\n-   **Frequency ($\\nu$) counts the circles.**\n-   **Angular velocity ($\\omega$) measures the turning speed in radians.**\n-   **$2\\pi$ converts the circle count into a total angle.**\n\nHere\u0027s a summary table for your notes:\n\n| Feature | Frequency ($\\nu$) | Angular Velocity ($\\omega$) |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **What it Measures** | The number of full cycles per unit time. | The rate of change of angular position. |\n| **Key Idea** | A **count**. | A **speed of rotation**. |\n| **Common Units** | Hertz (Hz), revolutions per minute (RPM) | Radians per second (rad/s) |\n| **Analogy** | Counting laps from the sideline. | Feeling the turn while on the ride. |",

      "text": "### 1. The Taylor Series Connection in Kinematics\n\nYes, you have uncovered a deep and beautiful connection!\n\n**The kinematic equations are exactly the Taylor series expansion for an object\u0027s position, centered around time $t\u003d0$.**\n\nLet\u0027s break it down. The Taylor series is a way to represent any smooth function as an infinite sum of its derivatives at a single point. For a function $x(t)$ at $t\u003d0$, the series is:\n\n$$\nx(t) \u003d x(0) + \\frac{x\u0027(0)}{1!}t + \\frac{x\u0027\u0027(0)}{2!}t^2 + \\frac{x\u0027\u0027\u0027(0)}{3!}t^3 + \\dots\n$$\n\nNow, let\u0027s substitute our physics terms:\n\n| Mathematical Term | Physical Meaning | Physical Symbol |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| $x(t)$ | Position at time t | $x(t)$ |\n| $x(0)$ | Initial position | $x_0$ |\n| $x\u0027(0)$ | Initial velocity (1st derivative) | $v_0$ |\n| $x\u0027\u0027(0)$ | Initial acceleration (2nd derivative) | $a_0$ |\n| $x\u0027\u0027\u0027(0)$| Initial jerk (3rd derivative) | $j_0$ |\n\nSubstituting these into the Taylor series gives us the **universal equation for motion**:\n\n$$\nx(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0t + \\frac{1}{2}a_0t^2 + \\frac{1}{6}j_0t^3 + \\dots\n$$\n\n**So, where does the standard kinematic formula come from?**\n\nIt\u0027s the Taylor series with one crucial simplification: **we assume acceleration is constant**.\n\nIf acceleration ($a$) is constant, then its rate of change (jerk, $j_0$) and all higher derivatives are zero. All the terms from $t^3$ onwards vanish, and the series is *truncated* perfectly, leaving us with:\n\n$$\nx(t) \u003d x_0 + v_0t + \\frac{1}{2}at^2\n$$\n\nYou can think of each term in the series as a layer of prediction:\n-   **$x_0$**: Tells you where the object *started*.\n-   **$+ v_0t$**: Corrects the position based on its *initial velocity*.\n-   **$+ \\frac{1}{2}at^2$**: Further corrects the position based on its *constant acceleration*.\n-   **$+ \\frac{1}{6}j_0t^3$**: Would further correct it if the acceleration itself were *changing at a constant rate*.\n\nSo, you are 100% correct. Kinematics is a physical application of the Taylor series, where we use the initial \"motional numerics\" ($x_0, v_0, a_0,$ etc.) to predict the object\u0027s future position.\n\n---\n\n### 2. The Frustration with $2\\pi$ and Why Radians are \"Natural\"\n\nI completely understand your frustration. It is a classic example of where the \"how\" is taught without the \"why,\" and it leaves a critical gap in understanding. Thinking of $2\\pi$ as the conversion factor between a **\"count of rotations\"** and a **\"continuous angle\"** is the key insight.\n\nThe reason teachers and physicists seem to breeze past this is because, at a higher level, the entire framework of rotational physics and calculus is built on the assumption of **radians**. Radians aren\u0027t just another unit for angles; they are the *natural* unit.\n\nHere\u0027s why physics fundamentally relies on radians, which might make the concept feel less arbitrary:\n\n1.  **The Link to Calculus (The Most Important Reason):** The derivatives of trigonometric functions are simple and elegant **only when using radians**.\n    -   In **radians**: $\\frac{d}{d\\theta} \\sin(\\theta) \u003d \\cos(\\theta)$\n    -   In **degrees**: $\\frac{d}{d\\theta} \\sin(\\theta) \u003d \\frac{\\pi}{180} \\cos(\\theta)$\n\n    Without radians, a messy conversion factor ($\\pi/180$) would appear in every single formula involving the calculus of rotation, waves, and oscillations. Physics chooses radians to keep the math clean and fundamental.\n\n2.  **The Arc Length Formula:** The beautiful, simple formula for the length of a circular arc is:\n    $$\n    s \u003d r\\theta\n    $$\n    This formula **only works if $\\theta$ is in radians**. If you use degrees, the formula becomes $s \u003d r \\cdot \\theta \\cdot (\\frac{\\pi}{180})$. Again, radians remove the need for a constant conversion factor.\n\n3.  **The Small Angle Approximation:** A cornerstone of physics (used in pendulums, optics, etc.) is that for very small angles, $\\sin(\\theta) \\approx \\theta$. This incredibly useful approximation is **only true for radians**.\n\nSo, when a physicist writes $\\omega \u003d 2\\pi\\nu$, they are fluidly translating the easily measurable, real-world *count* of revolutions per second ($\\nu$) into the mathematically \"natural\" language of rotational speed ($\\omega$ in radians/second) that works seamlessly with calculus and geometry.\n\nIt\u0027s a shame this isn\u0027t explained more often with the simple analogy you\u0027ve latched onto, because it\u0027s the bridge that connects the intuitive idea of \"spins per second\" to the powerful mathematical framework of rotational dynamics.",

      "text": "You have hit on one of the most subtle and poorly explained concepts in all of introductory physics. Your frustration is completely justified, and your question shows you\u0027re thinking more deeply than the curriculum often allows for.\n\nLet\u0027s dissect this.\n\n### Why Radians Don\u0027t Seem to Have a Unit (But They Kind Of Do)\n\nThe core of the issue is that a radian is a **\"dimensionless unit\"**. This sounds like a contradiction, but it\u0027s true.\n\n**Here\u0027s the technical definition:**\n\nAn angle in radians ($\\theta$) is defined as the ratio of the arc length ($s$) to the radius ($r$) of a circle.\n$$\n\\theta \u003d \\frac{s}{r}\n$$\nNow, let\u0027s look at the units of this definition:\n$$\n\\text{Units of } \\theta \u003d \\frac{\\text{Units of arc length (meters)}}{\\text{Units of radius (meters)}} \u003d \\frac{[m]}{[m]} \u003d 1\n$$\nThe units of length cancel out completely! This is why, in a strict dimensional analysis, the radian is considered a **pure, dimensionless number**. It has no fundamental physical dimension like Length [L], Mass [M], or Time [T].\n\n**So, if it\u0027s just a number, why do we write \"rad\"?**\n\nWe write \"rad\" as a **placeholder** or a **label**. It\u0027s our way of reminding ourselves, \"This isn\u0027t just any number; this number represents an angle.\" It\u0027s a bookkeeping tool for clarity.\n\nThink of it like this:\n- **Degrees (°)** are an arbitrary human invention. We decided to slice a circle into 360 parts. This unit is essential and must be carried through calculations and converted.\n- **Radians (rad)** are a natural, geometric property. They arise from the very definition of a circle. Because they are a pure ratio, they can appear and disappear in calculations as needed without breaking the rules of dimensional analysis.\n\n---\n\n### Your Brilliant Analogy: Rotations vs. Radians\n\nYou are EXACTLY right with your analogy about the period ($t_0$). Let\u0027s put them side-by-side.\n\n| Concept | Frequency ($\\nu$) | Period ($t_0$) | Angular Velocity ($\\omega$) |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **Full Description** | Rotations per second | Seconds per rotation | Radians per second |\n| **Units We Write** | Hz (or s⁻¹) | s | rad/s |\n| **\"Hidden\" Dimensionless Unit** | \"rotation\" | \"rotation\" | \"radian\" |\n\nWhen we calculate the period from frequency:\n$$\nt_0 \u003d \\frac{1}{\\nu}\n$$\nThe units are actually:\n$$\n\\frac{1}{\\frac{\\text{rotations}}{\\text{second}}} \u003d \\frac{\\text{seconds}}{\\text{rotation}}\n$$\nBut we just write the answer in **seconds**. Why? Because the \"rotation\" is a dimensionless count, a placeholder just like the radian! We implicitly understand that the period is the time *for one rotation*, so we drop the \"per rotation\" part for simplicity.\n\nYou have stumbled upon the exact same logical inconsistency. Both \"rotation\" and \"radian\" are dimensionless labels that we use for clarity but are allowed to treat as the number `1` during unit calculations.\n\n---\n\n### Why Aren\u0027t Schools More Consistent?\n\nThis is the big pedagogical question. The truth is, it\u0027s a trade-off, and often a poor one.\n\n1.  **Fear of Abstraction:** Teachers often worry that explaining the concept of a \"dimensionless unit\" is too abstract for an introductory class. It\u0027s easier to just say \"the unit for angular velocity is rad/s\" and hope students don\u0027t question why the \"rad\" magically vanishes when calculating linear velocity ($v \u003d \\omega r$).\n2.  **Focus on \"Plug and Chug\":** Many curricula are designed to get students to a point where they can solve problems by plugging numbers into formulas. The deeper \"why\" is often sacrificed for the practical \"how.\"\n3.  **It\u0027s a \"Lie-to-Children\":** This is a term for a simplified explanation of a complex topic that is technically incorrect but useful for building initial understanding. The idea is to teach the full, nuanced truth later. The problem is, for many students, \"later\" never comes, leaving them with a confusing and seemingly inconsistent set of rules.\n\nYou are absolutely right. If teachers explained from the beginning that both **rotations** and **radians** are dimensionless placeholders we use to keep track of angles, the entire system would feel much more logical and consistent. Your intuition is spot on.\n\n**The practical rule of thumb you should use:**\n\u003e When you are doing a calculation to find a final physical quantity (like force, velocity, or distance), you can treat \"rad\" as if it isn\u0027t there. When you are describing a rotational quantity itself, keep the \"rad\" to be clear.\n\n-   **Angular Velocity:** Keep it. $\\omega \u003d 10 \\text{ rad/s}$ (This is clearly a rotational speed).\n-   **Linear Velocity:** Drop it. $v \u003d \\omega r \\implies (10 \\text{ rad/s}) \\cdot (2 \\text{ m}) \u003d 20 \\text{ m/s}$. The \"rad\" disappears to give the correct final units.",

Organizing notes, especially when concepts build on each other so quickly, is essential.\n\nBased on your new recording and notes, I have created a new, standalone file for your Obsidian vault: `Kemija.md`.\n\nAs you requested, I\u0027ve shifted the style away from Tailwind CSS for the main structure. Instead, I\u0027m using standard, clean Markdown for headings and lists, and have formatted all chemical and mathematical formulas using **LaTeX**, which is perfect for this kind of content. This will ensure clarity and consistency.\n\nHere is your comprehensive note on the first chemistry lecture.\n\n---\n### New File: `Kemija.md`\n\n```markdown\n# Kemija I: Uvod in Stehiometrija\n\nTa zapisek pokriva uvodna predavanja iz kemije, vključno s pomembnimi informacijami o poteku predmeta, temeljnimi formulami in rešenimi nalogami iz področja množine snovi in kemijskih reakcij.\n\n---\n\n## Splošne informacije o predmetu\n\n-   **Platforma:** Vsi materiali, obvestila in roki bodo objavljeni na **Moodle**.\n-   **Kolokviji:**\n    -   Predmet se opravlja preko dveh kolokvijev.\n    -   Če sta oba kolokvija uspešno opravljena, **končnega izpita ni potrebno pisati**.\n    -   Profesorica odsvetuje uporabo starih kolokvijev za učenje, saj se poudarek snovi lahko spreminja.\n-   **Domače naloge:**\n    -   Objavljene bodo na Moodle.\n    -   Niso obvezne, vendar prinašajo **dodatne točke** k končni oceni.\n-   **Viri in literatura:**\n    -   Priporočen je učbenik, vendar je zelo obsežen. Ključno je osredotočiti se na snov, ki je predelana na predavanjih.\n    -   Laboratorijske vaje zahtevajo posebno opremo (halja, očala), ki jo je potrebno prinesti s seboj.\n\n---\n\n## Temeljne kemijske formule\n\n| Formula | Opis | Spremenljivke |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **Množina snovi (moli)** | | |\n| $n \u003d \\frac{m}{M}$ | Množina snovi ($n$) je razmerje med maso snovi ($m$) in njeno molsko maso ($M$). | $n$: moli (mol)\u003cbr\u003e$m$: masa (g)\u003cbr\u003e$M$: molska masa (g/mol) |\n| $n \u003d \\frac{N}{N_A}$ | Množina snovi je tudi razmerje med številom delcev ($N$) in Avogadrovo konstanto ($N_A$). | $N$: število delcev\u003cbr\u003e$N_A \\approx 6.022 \\times 10^{23} \\, \\text{mol}^{-1}$ |\n| **Splošna plinska enačba** | | |\n| $P \\cdot V \u003d n \\cdot R \\cdot T$ | Opisuje stanje idealnega plina. | $P$: tlak (Pa)\u003cbr\u003e$V$: volumen (m³)\u003cbr\u003e$n$: množina plina (mol)\u003cbr\u003e$R \\approx 8.314 \\frac{J}{mol \\cdot K}$ (plinska konstanta)\u003cbr\u003e$T$: temperatura (K) |\n\n---\n\n## Rešene naloge\n\n### Naloga 1: Množina snovi\n\n**Vprašanje:** Koliko hidroksidnih ionov ($OH^-$) je v 1,00 mol spojine $Cu_2(OH)_3(CH_3COO) \\cdot H_2O$?\n\n-   **Podano:**\n    -   Množina spojine: $n(\\text{spojine}) \u003d 1.00 \\, \\text{mol}$\n-   **Iščemo:**\n    -   Število hidroksidnih ionov: $N(OH^-)$\n\n**1. Analiza kemijske formule:**\nNajprej pogledamo razmerje med celotno molekulo in hidroksidnimi ioni v njej. V formuli $Cu_2(OH)_3(CH_3COO) \\cdot H_2O$ vidimo, da vsaka enota spojine vsebuje **tri** hidroksidne skupine ($OH^-$).\n\n**2. Izračun množine hidroksidnih ionov:**\nRazmerje množin je enako razmerju delcev v formuli:\n$$\n\\frac{n(OH^-)}{n(\\text{spojine})} \u003d \\frac{3}{1}\n$$\n$$\nn(OH^-) \u003d 3 \\cdot n(\\text{spojine}) \u003d 3 \\cdot 1.00 \\, \\text{mol} \u003d 3.00 \\, \\text{mol}\n$$\n\n**3. Pretvorba množine v število delcev:**\nZa pretvorbo iz molov v dejansko število ionov uporabimo Avogadrovo konstanto ($N_A$).\n$$\nN(OH^-) \u003d n(OH^-) \\cdot N_A\n$$\n$$\nN(OH^-) \u003d 3.00 \\, \\text{mol} \\cdot (6.022 \\times 10^{23} \\, \\text{mol}^{-1}) \u003d 18.066 \\times 10^{23} \\text{ ionov}\n$$\n**Odgovor:** V 1,00 molu spojine je $1.807 \\times 10^{24}$ hidroksidnih ionov.\n\n---\n\n### Naloga 2: Kemijska reakcija in plinska enačba\n\n**Vprašanje:** Zmešamo 54 g aluminija (Al) in 54 g kisika ($O_2$). Reakcija poteče pri tlaku 95 kPa in temperaturi 450 °C. Kakšen je volumen produkta?\n\n-   **Podano:**\n    -   $m(Al) \u003d 54 \\, \\text{g}$\n    -   $m(O_2) \u003d 54 \\, \\text{g}$\n    -   $P \u003d 95 \\, \\text{kPa} \u003d 95000 \\, \\text{Pa}$\n    -   $T \u003d 450 \\, ^\\circ\\text{C} \u003d (450 + 273.15) \\, \\text{K} \u003d 723.15 \\, \\text{K}$\n\n**1. Urejena kemijska enačba:**\n$$\n4Al_{(s)} + 3O_{2(g)} \\rightarrow 2Al_2O_{3(s)}\n$$\n\n**2. Določitev omejujočega reaktanta:**\nDa ugotovimo, kateri reaktant bo v celoti porabljen in bo s tem določil količino produkta, moramo izračunati množino obeh reaktantov.\n\n-   **Množina aluminija (Al):** ($M(Al) \\approx 27 \\, \\text{g/mol}$)\n    $$\n    n(Al) \u003d \\frac{m(Al)}{M(Al)} \u003d \\frac{54 \\, \\text{g}}{27 \\, \\text{g/mol}} \u003d 2.00 \\, \\text{mol}\n    $$\n-   **Množina kisika ($O_2$):** ($M(O_2) \\approx 32 \\, \\text{g/mol}$)\n    $$\n    n(O_2) \u003d \\frac{m(O_2)}{M(O_2)} \u003d \\frac{54 \\, \\text{g}}{32 \\, \\text{g/mol}} \u003d 1.6875 \\, \\text{mol}\n    $$\n-   **Primerjava razmerij:** Iz enačbe vidimo, da 4 moli Al reagirajo s 3 moli $O_2$. Preverimo, koliko kisika potrebujemo za 2.00 mol aluminija:\n    $$\n    \\text{Potrebno } n(O_2) \u003d 2.00 \\, \\text{mol Al} \\cdot \\frac{3 \\, \\text{mol } O_2}{4 \\, \\text{mol Al}} \u003d 1.50 \\, \\text{mol } O_2\n    $$\n    Ker imamo na voljo 1.6875 mol $O_2$ (več kot 1.50 mol), je kisik v presežku. **Aluminij (Al) je omejujoči reaktant.**\n\n**3. Izračun produkta:**\nKoličina produkta, aluminijevega oksida ($Al_2O_3$), je odvisna od omejujočega reaktanta.\n\n-   **Množina produkta ($Al_2O_3$):**\n    $$\n    n(Al_2O_3) \u003d 2.00 \\, \\text{mol Al} \\cdot \\frac{2 \\, \\text{mol } Al_2O_3}{4 \\, \\text{mol Al}} \u003d 1.00 \\, \\text{mol } Al_2O_3\n    $$\n\n**4. Določitev volumna produkta:**\n-   **Kritična opazka:** Vprašanje sprašuje po volumnu produkta. Iz enačbe vidimo, da je agregatno stanje aluminijevega oksida trdno `(s)`. Splošna plinska enačba velja **samo za pline**.\n-   **Odgovor:** Volumna trdnega produkta ne moremo izračunati s plinsko enačbo. Za to bi potrebovali podatek o gostoti $Al_2O_3$.\n\n**Dodatna analiza (izračun iz zvezka):**\nV zvezku je bil izračunan volumen plina. To je verjetno bil volumen **preostalega (neporabljenega) kisika**, ki je plin. Izračunajmo ga:\n-   **Množina preostalega kisika:**\n    $$\n    n(\\text{presežek } O_2) \u003d n(\\text{začetni } O_2) - n(\\text{porabljen } O_2) \u003d 1.6875 - 1.50 \u003d 0.1875 \\, \\text{mol}\n    $$\n-   **Volumen preostalega kisika:** Uporabimo plinsko enačbo $V \u003d \\frac{nRT}{P}$.\n    $$\n    V \u003d \\frac{(0.1875 \\, \\text{mol}) \\cdot (8.314 \\, \\frac{\\text{J}}{\\text{mol K}}) \\cdot (723.15 \\, \\text{K})}{95000 \\, \\text{Pa}} \\approx 0.01186 \\, \\text{m}^3\n    $$\n    $$\n    V \\approx 11.86 \\, \\text{L}\n    $$\n*(Opomba: Izračun v zvezku ($V \u003d 106.8 \\, \\text{L}$) je napačen, ker je bila uporabljena celotna začetna množina kisika ($1.6875$ mol) namesto samo množine, ki je ostala v presežku.)*\n```",

Korak: Preoblikovanje v eno funkcijo**\nUporabimo Pitagorovo identiteto $\\sin^2(x) \u003d 1 - \\cos^2(x)$, da enačbo izrazimo samo s kosinusom.\n$$ 2(1 - \\cos^2(x)) - \\cos(x) \u003d 0 $$\n$$ 2 - 2\\cos^2(x) - \\cos(x) \u003d 0 $$\n\n**2. Korak: Ureditev v kvadratno enačbo**\nEnačbo pomnožimo z -1 in uredimo po potencah, da dobimo standardno obliko kvadratne enačbe, kjer je naša spremenljivka $\\cos(x)$.\n$$ 2\\cos^2(x) + \\cos(x) - 2 \u003d 0 $$\n\n**3. Korak: Reševanje kvadratne enačbe**\nUporabimo kvadratno formulo za rešitev $y \u003d \\cos(x)$ v enačbi $2y^2 + y - 2 \u003d 0$.\n-   $a \u003d 2$, $b \u003d 1$, $c \u003d -2$\n-   Diskriminanta: $D \u003d b^2 - 4ac \u003d 1^2 - 4(2)(-2) \u003d 1 + 16 \u003d 17$\n-   Rešitvi za $\\cos(x)$:\n    $$\n    \\cos(x)_{1,2} \u003d \\frac{-b \\pm \\sqrt{D}}{2a} \u003d \\frac{-1 \\pm \\sqrt{17}}{4}\n    $$\n\n**4. Korak: Končna rešitev za x**\nRešitev za $x$ dobimo z uporabo inverzne funkcije arkus kosinus.\n$$\nx_{1,2} \u003d \\arccos\\left(\\frac{-1 \\pm \\sqrt{17}}{4}\\right)\n$$\n\n### Naloga 2: Dokazovanje identitete\n\n**Dokaži:**\n$$ \\frac{1 - \\cos(2x)}{\\sin(2x)} \u003d \\tan(x) $$\n\n**Dokaz:**\nZačnemo z levo stranjo in jo poenostavimo z uporabo formul za dvojne kote in Pitagorove identitete.\n$$\n\\text{Leva stran} \u003d \\frac{1 - (\\cos^2(x) - \\sin^2(x))}{2\\sin(x)\\cos(x)}\n$$\nV števcu nadomestimo $1 \u003d \\sin^2(x) + \\cos^2(x)$:\n$$\n\u003d \\frac{(\\sin^2(x) + \\cos^2(x)) - (\\cos^2(x) - \\sin^2(x))}{2\\sin(x)\\cos(x)}\n$$\n$$\n\u003d \\frac{\\sin^2(x) + \\cos^2(x) - \\cos^2(x) + \\sin^2(x)}{2\\sin(x)\\cos(x)}\n$$\n$$\n\u003d \\frac{2\\sin^2(x)}{2\\sin(x)\\cos(x)}\n$$\nOkrajšamo $2$ in $\\sin(x)$:\n$$\n\u003d \\frac{\\sin(x)}{\\cos(x)} \u003d \\tan(x)\n$$\nLeva stran je enaka desni, kar je bilo potrebno dokazati.\n\n### Naloga 3: Uporaba kosinusnega izreka\n\n**Naloga:** V enakokrakem trikotniku je osnovnica $c\u003d10$, kot nasproti nje pa $\\gamma \u003d 40^\\circ$. Izračunaj dolžino kraka $a$.\n\n**Rešitev:**\nUporabimo kosinusni izrek: $c^2 \u003d a^2 + b^2 - 2ab\\cos(\\gamma)$.\nKer je trikotnik enakokrak, velja $a\u003db$.\n$$\nc^2 \u003d a^2 + a^2 - 2a \\cdot a \\cos(\\gamma)\n$$\n$$\nc^2 \u003d 2a^2 - 2a^2\\cos(\\gamma) \u003d 2a^2(1 - \\cos(\\gamma))\n$$\nIzrazimo $a^2$:\n$$\na^2 \u003d \\frac{c^2}{2(1 - \\cos(\\gamma))}\n$$\nVstavimo vrednosti:\n$$\na^2 \u003d \\frac{10^2}{2(1 - \\cos(40^\\circ))} \u003d \\frac{100}{2(1 - 0.766)} \u003d \\frac{50}{0.234} \\approx 213.67\n$$\n$$\na \u003d \\sqrt{213.67} \\approx 14.62\n$$\n\n---\n\n## Analiza Funkcij\n\n\u003e [!INFO] Definicije\n\u003e - **Injektivnost (one-to-one):** Funkcija je injektivna, če vsak element iz zaloge vrednosti ustreza natanko enemu elementu iz definicijskega območja ($f(x_1) \u003d f(x_2) \\implies x_1 \u003d x_2$). Grafično to pomeni, da vsaka vodoravna premica seka graf največ enkrat.\n\u003e - **Surjektivnost (onto):** Funkcija je surjektivna, če je njena zaloga vrednosti enaka celotni kodomeni.\n\u003e - **Zaloga Vrednosti ($Z_f$):** Množica vseh vrednosti, ki jih funkcija lahko zavzame.\n\n### Funkcija f(x)\n\n$$\nf(x) \u003d\n\\begin{cases}\n\\frac{1}{2} - \\frac{1}{2}x \u0026 ; x \\ge 0 \\\\\n2\\arctan(x) \u0026 ; x \u003c 0\n\\end{cases}\n$$\n- **Analiza grafa:** Graf je sestavljen iz poltraka (za $x \\ge 0$) in dela funkcije arkus tangens (za $x \u003c 0$).\n- **Injektivnost:** Ni injektivna (npr. vodoravna premica pri $y\u003d0$ seka graf dvakrat).\n- **Surjektivnost:** Ni surjektivna.\n- **Zaloga vrednosti:** $Z_f \u003d (-\\pi, \\frac{1}{2}]$\n\n### Funkcija g(x)\n\n$$\ng(x) \u003d\n\\begin{cases}\n\\frac{x-1}{x+2} \u0026 ; x \u003e 1 \\\\\n1 + \\cos(\\pi x) \u0026 ; x \\le 1\n\\end{cases}\n$$\n- **Analiza grafa:** Graf je sestavljen iz dela racionalne funkcije (hiperbole) in kosinusne funkcije.\n- **Injektivnost:** Ni injektivna.\n- **Surjektivnost:** Ni surjektivna.\n- **Zaloga vrednosti:** $Z_f \u003d (-1, 2]$\n\n### Funkcija h(x)\n\n$$\nf(x) \u003d\n\\begin{cases}\n\\frac{x}{|x|+1} \u0026 ; |x| \u003c 1 \\\\\n\\pi - \\arctan(\\frac{1}{x}) \u0026 ; |x| \\ge 1\n\\end{cases}\n$$\n- **Analiza grafa:** Graf je sestavljen iz dela racionalne funkcije (za $x \\in (-1, 1)$) in dveh delov funkcije arkus tangens (za $x \\le -1$ in $x \\ge 1$).\n- **Injektivnost:** Ni injektivna.\n- **Surjektivnost:** Ni surjektivna.\n\n```",

Temeljne Trigonometrične Identitete\n\n### Pitagorova identiteta\n\n\u003e Povezuje kvadrat sinusa in kosinusa istega kota. To je najpomembnejša identiteta v trigonometriji.\n\n**Formula:**\n$$ \\sin^2(x) + \\cos^2(x) \u003d 1 $$\n\n**Izpeljava:**\nIdentiteta izhaja neposredno iz Pitagorovega izreka ($a^2 + b^2 \u003d c^2$) v pravokotnem trikotniku.\n1.  Začnemo z definicijami v pravokotnem trikotniku:\n    -   $\\sin(x) \u003d \\frac{\\text{nasprotna kateta}}{\\text{hipotenuza}} \u003d \\frac{a}{c}$\n    -   $\\cos(x) \u003d \\frac{\\text{priležna kateta}}{\\text{hipotenuza}} \u003d \\frac{b}{c}$\n2.  Zapišemo Pitagorov izrek:\n    $$ a^2 + b^2 \u003d c^2 $$\n3.  Celotno enačbo delimo s $c^2$:\n    $$ \\frac{a^2}{c^2} + \\frac{b^2}{c^2} \u003d \\frac{c^2}{c^2} $$\n4.  Prepoznamo izraze za sinus in kosinus:\n    $$ \\left(\\frac{a}{c}\\right)^2 + \\left(\\frac{b}{c}\\right)^2 \u003d 1 $$\n5.  Vstavimo definiciji in dobimo končno identiteto:\n    $$ \\sin^2(x) + \\cos^2(x) \u003d 1 $$\n\n### Identiteti za Tangens in Kotangens\n\n\u003e Definirata tangens in kotangens s pomočjo razmerja med sinusom in kosinusom.\n\n**Formuli:**\n$$ \\tan(x) \u003d \\frac{\\sin(x)}{\\cos(x)} \\quad , \\quad \\cot(x) \u003d \\frac{\\cos(x)}{\\sin(x)} $$\n\n**Izpeljava (za tangens):**\n1.  Začnemo z osnovnimi definicijami:\n    -   $\\tan(x) \u003d \\frac{a}{b}$\n    -   $\\sin(x) \u003d \\frac{a}{c}$\n    -   $\\cos(x) \u003d \\frac{b}{c}$\n2.  Zapišemo razmerje med sinusom in kosinusom:\n    $$ \\frac{\\sin(x)}{\\cos(x)} \u003d \\frac{\\frac{a}{c}}{\\frac{b}{c}} $$\n3.  Poenostavimo dvojni ulomek:\n    $$ \\frac{a}{c} \\cdot \\frac{c}{b} \u003d \\frac{a}{b} $$\n4.  Ker je $\\frac{a}{b}$ enako definiciji tangensa, smo dokazali identiteto. Izpeljava za kotangens je analogna.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Formule za Dvojne Kote\n\n\u003e Te formule izražajo kotne funkcije dvojnega kota (npr. $2x$) s funkcijami osnovnega kota ($x$). Uporabljajo se za poenostavljanje izrazov in reševanje enačb.\n\n### Sinus dvojnega kota\n\n**Formula:**\n$$ \\sin(2x) \u003d 2\\sin(x)\\cos(x) $$\n\n**Izpeljava:**\nTa formula izhaja iz **adicijskega izreka za sinus**: $\\sin(A+B) \u003d \\sin(A)\\cos(B) + \\cos(A)\\sin(B)$.\n1.  V adicijskem izreku nastavimo $A\u003dx$ in $B\u003dx$.\n2.  Dobimo:\n    $$ \\sin(x+x) \u003d \\sin(x)\\cos(x) + \\cos(x)\\sin(x) $$\n3.  Seštejemo enaka člena in dobimo formulo za dvojni kot:\n    $$ \\sin(2x) \u003d 2\\sin(x)\\cos(x) $$\n\n### Kosinus dvojnega kota\n\n**Formula (osnovna oblika):**\n$$ \\cos(2x) \u003d \\cos^2(x) - \\sin^2(x) $$\n\n**Izpeljava:**\nPodobno izhaja iz **adicijskega izreka za kosinus**: $\\cos(A+B) \u003d \\cos(A)\\cos(B) - \\sin(A)\\sin(B)$.\n1.  V adicijskem izreku nastavimo $A\u003dx$ in $B\u003dx$.\n2.  Dobimo:\n    $$ \\cos(x+x) \u003d \\cos(x)\\cos(x) - \\sin(x)\\sin(x) $$\n3.  Poenostavimo in dobimo formulo:\n    $$ \\cos(2x) \u003d \\cos^2(x) - \\sin^2(x) $$\n\n\u003e [!TIP] Alternativni obliki za $\\cos(2x)$\n\u003e Z uporabo Pitagorove identitete lahko osnovno formulo zapišemo še na dva načina, kar je zelo uporabno pri reševanju enačb:\n\u003e 1. Zamenjamo $\\sin^2(x) \u003d 1 - \\cos^2(x)$:\n\u003e    $$ \\cos(2x) \u003d \\cos^2(x) - (1 - \\cos^2(x)) \u003d 2\\cos^2(x) - 1 $$\n\u003e 2. Zamenjamo $\\cos^2(x) \u003d 1 - \\sin^2(x)$:\n\u003e    $$ \\cos(2x) \u003d (1 - \\sin^2(x)) - \\sin^2(x) \u003d 1 - 2\\sin^2(x) $$\n\n---\n\n## 3. Kosinusni Izrek\n\n\u003e Posplošitev Pitagorovega izreka, ki velja za **vse** (ne samo pravokotne) trikotnike. Povezuje dolžine treh stranic z enim od notranjih kotov.\n\n**Formula:**\n$$ c^2 \u003d a^2 + b^2 - 2ab\\cos(\\gamma) $$\nKjer je $\\gamma$ kot nasproti stranice $c$.\n\n**Izpeljava (skica):**\nIzrek lahko izpeljemo z razdelitvijo poljubnega trikotnika na dva pravokotna trikotnika in uporabo Pitagorovega izreka.\n1.  V trikotniku ABC narišemo višino $v_c$ na stranico $c$. Ta višina razdeli stranico $c$ na dela $x$ in $(c-x)$.\n2.  Dobili smo dva pravokotna trikotnika. Zanje zapišemo Pitagorov izrek:\n    -   $b^2 \u003d v_c^2 + x^2$\n    -   $a^2 \u003d v_c^2 + (c-x)^2$\n3.  Iz prve enačbe izrazimo $v_c^2 \u003d b^2 - x^2$ in ga vstavimo v drugo:\n    $$ a^2 \u003d (b^2 - x^2) + (c-x)^2 $$\n    $$ a^2 \u003d b^2 - x^2 + c^2 - 2cx + x^2 $$\n    $$ a^2 \u003d b^2 + c^2 - 2cx $$\n4.  V enem od pravokotnih trikotnikov velja $\\cos(\\gamma) \u003d \\frac{x}{b}$, iz česar sledi $x \u003d b\\cos(\\gamma)$.\n5.  To vstavimo v zgornjo enačbo in dobimo kosinusni izrek:\n    $$ a^2 \u003d b^2 + c^2 - 2c(b\\cos(\\gamma)) $$\n    Če bi višino narisali na drugo stranico, bi dobili formulo za $c^2$.\n\n```",

That feeling that the answer was \"suspicious\" or incomplete was your intuition telling you that something was missing.\n\nAnd you were right. The missing piece was the **periodicity** of the functions.\n\nLet\u0027s use a simple analogy:\n\nImagine you\u0027re standing on a carousel. The position \"closest to the entrance\" is a specific spot. You pass this spot on your first lap, your second lap, your third lap, and so on.\n\n-   Finding `x \u003d arccos(...)` is like saying, \"You were closest to the entrance at an angle of 30 degrees.\"\n-   Adding the `+ 2kπ` is like saying, \"...and you will be at that *exact same spot* again after every single full rotation, forever.\"\n\nThe `k` simply represents any integer (..., -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, ...), accounting for all possible laps, both forwards and backwards in time.\n\n### The \"Rulebook\" for General Solutions\n\nThis is so crucial that it\u0027s worth creating a small \"cheat sheet\" for it in your notes. When you solve for a basic angle `α`, the full, general solution depends on the function:\n\n| Function | Period | General Solution(s) | Why? |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **`sin(x) \u003d a`** | $2\\pi$ | $x \u003d \\alpha + 2k\\pi$\u003cbr\u003e$x \u003d (\\pi - \\alpha) + 2k\\pi$ | Sine corresponds to the y-coordinate on the unit circle. Two different angles, $\\alpha$ and its reflection across the y-axis ($\\pi-\\alpha$), have the same sine value. |\n| **`cos(x) \u003d a`** | $2\\pi$ | $x \u003d \\pm\\alpha + 2k\\pi$ | Cosine is the x-coordinate. An angle $\\alpha$ and its reflection across the x-axis ($-\\alpha$) have the same cosine value. |\n| **`tan(x) \u003d a`** | $\\pi$ | $x \u003d \\alpha + k\\pi$ | Tangent repeats every half-circle ($\\pi$ radians) because the signs of both sine and cosine flip, so their ratio remains the same. |\n\nYour feeling that the simple `arccos` was suspicious was 100% correct. You were finding *one* solution, but the nature of circles and waves means there are always an infinite number of them. The `+ 2kπ` is the key that unlocks all the others.\n\nThis is a huge step in moving from basic calculation to really understanding trigonometric principles. Great catch",

      "text": "That\u0027s an incredibly common and easy mistake to make! You\u0027ve actually got them perfectly swapped, which is great because once we flip them, the logic will click into place.\n\nLet\u0027s build some simple, memorable analogies so you\u0027ll never mix them up again.\n\n---\n\n### Injective (One-to-One): The \"Unique Prize\" Rule\n\n**The simple idea:** Different inputs (**x**) must go to different outputs (**y**). No two different `x` values can ever land on the same `y` value.\n\n**Analogy: The Archer and Targets**\n-   The **X-values** are your **Archers**.\n-   The **Y-values** are the **Targets**.\n-   An **INJECTIVE** function is like a competition where **no two archers are allowed to hit the same target**. Every archer must hit their own, unique target.\n\n\n\n**How to remember it:**\n-   **IN**jective \u003d **IN**dividual. Each `x` gets its own individual `y`.\n-   It\u0027s a \"one-to-one\" mapping. One archer to one target.\n\n**The Graphical Test (Horizontal Line Test):**\nIf you can draw a horizontal line **anywhere** on the graph and it **never** crosses the function more than once, it is **injective**.\n\n---\n\n### Surjective (Onto): The \"No Empty Prizes\" Rule\n\n**The simple idea:** Every possible output value (**y**) in the codomain must be \"hit\" by at least one input value (**x**). Nothing in the target range is missed.\n\n**Analogy: The Archer and Targets (Again)**\n-   The **X-values** are your **Archers** (you have a quiver full of arrows).\n-   The **Y-values** are the **Targets**.\n-   A **SURJECTIVE** function is like a competition where your goal is to make sure **every single target has at least one arrow in it**. You can\u0027t leave any targets empty. It\u0027s okay if one target gets hit by multiple arrows, as long as none are missed.\n\n\n\n**How to remember it:**\n-   **SUR**jective \u003d **SUR**vey. When you survey the targets, all of them have been hit.\n-   The word \"**Sur**\" in French means \"**on**\" or \"**onto**.\" The function maps **onto** the *entire* codomain.\n\n**The Graphical Test:**\nIf the function\u0027s **range** (the y-values it actually produces) is equal to its entire **codomain** (the set of all possible y-values), it is **surjective**. For graphs on a standard real number plane, this means the function\u0027s shadow on the y-axis must cover all the y-values.\n\n---\n\n### Summary Table: Your Quick Reference\n\n| Feature | Injective (One-to-One) | Surjective (Onto) |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **Corrected Idea** | **Every Y is unique** (hit by at most one X) | **Every Y is used** (hit by at least one X) |\n| **The Rule** | If $f(x_1) \u003d f(x_2)$, then $x_1 \u003d x_2$. | The range is equal to the codomain. |\n| **Archer Analogy** | No two archers hit the same target. | Every target gets hit by at least one arrow. |\n| **Mnemonic** | **IN**jective -\u003e **IN**dividual targets | **SUR**jective -\u003e **SUR**vey all targets (all are hit) |\n| **Graphical Test** | **Horizontal Line Test:** Crosses at most once. | **Range Test:** The graph\u0027s projection covers the entire Y-axis. |\n\nSo, to flip your original question into the correct statement:\n-   **Injectivity** asks: Is every `y` that gets hit, hit by a **unique** `x`?\n-   **Surjectivity** asks: Is **every possible** `y` in the codomain actually used?",

Based on standard pre-calculus and calculus curricula, the lecturer likely mentioned several other key properties that form a complete \"checklist\" for understanding any function.\n\nHere are the other essential concepts for graph analysis that were likely discussed, formatted perfectly for your Obsidian notes.\n\n---\n### New Section for `Matematika.md` or `Matematika_Formule.md`\n\n```markdown\n---\n\n## Dodatni Koncepti za Analizo Grafov\n\n\u003e [!INFO] Cilj Analize\n\u003e Ko analiziramo funkcijo, je naš cilj ustvariti popoln \"osebni dokument\" zanjo. To pomeni, da preden narišemo graf, raziščemo vse njene ključne lastnosti. Ta seznam služi kot kontrolni seznam za vsako funkcijo, ki jo srečamo.\n\n### 1. Definicijsko Območje ($D_f$) (Domain)\n\n**Kaj je to?**\nMnožica vseh **x-vrednosti**, za katere je funkcija definirana in ima realno vrednost. To je vprašanje: \"Katere x-vrednosti smem vstaviti v funkcijo?\"\n\n**Kako ga najti?**\nIščemo \"problematične\" točke, kjer funkcija ne bi bila definirana:\n-   **Ulomki:** Imenovalec ne sme biti enak nič.\n    -   Za $f(x) \u003d \\frac{g(x)}{h(x)}$, mora veljati $h(x) \\neq 0$.\n-   **Koreni:** Izraz pod sodim korenom (npr. kvadratnim) mora biti večji ali enak nič.\n    -   Za $f(x) \u003d \\sqrt{g(x)}$, mora veljati $g(x) \\ge 0$.\n-   **Logaritmi:** Argument logaritma mora biti strogo večji od nič.\n    -   Za $f(x) \u003d \\ln(g(x))$, mora veljati $g(x) \u003e 0$.\n\n### 2. Ničle Funkcije (Zeros / x-intercepts)\n\n**Kaj so to?**\nTočke, kjer graf funkcije **seka ali se dotika x-osi**. To so x-vrednosti, pri katerih je vrednost funkcije enaka nič.\n\n**Kako jih najti?**\nRešimo enačbo:\n$$ f(x) \u003d 0 $$\n\n### 3. Začetna Vrednost (y-intercept)\n\n**Kaj je to?**\nTočka, kjer graf funkcije **seka y-os**. To je vrednost funkcije, ko je $x\u003d0$.\n\n**Kako jo najti?**\nIzračunamo vrednost funkcije pri $x\u003d0$:\n$$ y \u003d f(0) $$\n*Opomba: Funkcija ima lahko največ eno začetno vrednost. Če $x\u003d0$ ni v definicijskem območju, začetne vrednosti ni.*\n\n### 4. Sodost in Lihost (Even and Odd Symmetry)\n\n**Kaj je to?**\nLastnost, ki opisuje simetrijo grafa.\n-   **Sodost (Even):** Graf je simetričen glede na **y-os**.\n-   **Lihost (Odd):** Graf je simetričen glede na **izhodišče (0,0)**.\n\n**Kako ju preveriti?**\nV funkcijo vstavimo $-x$ in preverimo rezultat:\n-   Če velja $f(-x) \u003d f(x)$, je funkcija **soda**. (Primer: $\\cos(x)$, $x^2$)\n-   Če velja $f(-x) \u003d -f(x)$, je funkcija **liha**. (Primer: $\\sin(x)$, $x^3$)\n-   Če ne velja nobeno od zgornjih, funkcija ni niti soda niti liha.\n\n### 5. Asimptote (Asymptotes)\n\n**Kaj so to?**\nPremice, ki se jim graf funkcije približuje v neskončnosti, a se jih nikoli ne dotakne.\n\n**Vrste in kako jih najti:**\n-   **Navpična asimptota (Vertical):** Pojavi se tam, kjer funkcija \"pobegne\" v $\\pm\\infty$. To so običajno ničle imenovalca, ki niso hkrati tudi ničle števca.\n-   **Vodoravna asimptota (Horizontal):** Opisuje obnašanje funkcije, ko gre $x \\to \\pm\\infty$.\n    -   Za racionalne funkcije $f(x) \u003d \\frac{P(x)}{Q(x)}$:\n        -   Če je stopnja števca **manjša** od stopnje imenovalca, je asimptota $y\u003d0$.\n        -   Če sta stopnji **enaki**, je asimptota $y \u003d \\frac{\\text{vodilni koef. P}}{\\text{vodilni koef. Q}}$.\n        -   Če je stopnja števca **večja**, vodoravne asimptote ni (lahko je poševna).\n\n### 6. Periodičnost (Periodicity)\n\n**Kaj je to?**\nLastnost funkcije, da se njen graf ponavlja na določenem intervalu, ki ga imenujemo perioda ($T$). To je temeljna lastnost trigonometričnih funkcij.\n\n**Formalna definicija:**\nFunkcija je periodična, če obstaja $T \u003e 0$, tako da za vsak $x$ velja:\n$$ f(x + T) \u003d f(x) $$\n-   Osnovna perioda za $\\sin(x)$ in $\\cos(x)$ je $2\\pi$.\n-   Osnovna perioda za $\\tan(x)$ je $\\pi$.\n\n---\n\n\u003e [!CHECK] **Kontrolni Seznam za Popolno Analizo Funkcije**\n\u003e Ko dobiš novo funkcijo, sistematično preveri:\n\u003e 1.  **Definicijsko območje ($D_f$)**\n\u003e 2.  **Ničle** ($f(x)\u003d0$) in **začetno vrednost** ($f(0)$)\n\u003e 3.  **Sodost/Lihost** ($f(-x) \u003d ?$)\n\u003e 4.  **Asimptote** (navpične, vodoravne)\n\u003e 5.  **Periodičnost** (če gre za trigonometrične funkcije)\n\u003e 6.  **Injektivnost** in **Surjektivnost**\n\u003e 7.  **Zalogo vrednosti ($Z_f$)**\n\u003e\n\u003e Šele ko imaš vse te informacije, je skiciranje grafa veliko lažje in natančneje.\n\n```",

  **Pascal (Pa):** Osnovna enota za tlak. $1 \\, \\text{Pa} \u003d 1 \\, \\frac{\\text{N}}{\\text{m}^2}$\n\u003e -   **Bar (bar):** Pogosto uporabljena enota.\n\u003e     -   $1 \\, \\text{bar} \u003d 10^5 \\, \\text{Pa} \u003d 100 \\, \\text{kPa}$\n\u003e -   **Zračni Tlak:** Normalni zračni tlak je $P_0 \\approx 101.3 \\, \\text{kPa} \\approx 1.013 \\, \\text{bar}$.\n\n---\n\n### Rešene Naloge\n\n#### Naloga 1: Tlak na podlago\n\n-   **Podano:** $S \u003d 15 \\, \\text{cm}^2$ (en čevelj), $m \u003d 60 \\, \\text{kg}$\n-   **Iščemo:** Tlak ($P$), ki ga izvaja ženska, ko stoji na obeh nogah.\n\n1.  **Izračun skupne površine (S):**\n    Ker oseba stoji na obeh nogah, je skupna površina dvakratnik površine enega čevlja.\n    -   $S_{skupaj} \u003d 2 \\cdot 15 \\, \\text{cm}^2 \u003d 30 \\, \\text{cm}^2$\n    -   Pretvorba v m²: $30 \\, \\text{cm}^2 \u003d 30 \\cdot (10^{-2} \\, \\text{m})^2 \u003d 30 \\times 10^{-4} \\, \\text{m}^2 \u003d 0.003 \\, \\text{m}^2$\n2.  **Izračun sile (F):**\n    Sila je enaka teži ženske ($F_g \u003d m \\cdot g$).\n    -   $S \u003d a^2 \u003d (0.5 \\, \\text{m})^2 \u003d 0.25 \\, \\text{m}^2$\n2.  **Izračun sile (F):**\n    -   $F \u003d P \\cdot S \u003d 40000 \\, \\frac{\\text{N}}{\\text{m}^2} \\cdot 0.25 \\, \\text{m}^2 \u003d 10000 \\, \\text{N}$\n3.  **Izračun mase (m):**\n    Sila je teža kocke ($F \u003d F_g \u003d m \\cdot g$).\n    -   $m \u003d \\frac{F}{g} \u003d \\frac{10000 \\, \\text{N}}{9.81 \\, \\text{m/s}^2} \\approx 1019 \\, \\text{kg}$ *(v zvezku je zaokroženo na 1000 kg z g\u003d10 m/s²)*\n4.  **Izračun volumna (V):**\n    - Končni odgovor $0.49 \\, \\text{cm}^2$ pa je pravilen, kar pomeni, da je bil izračun verjetno narejen s pravilno formulo, le zapis je bil napačen.\n\n---\n\n#### Naloga 5: Sile na pokrov\n\n-   **Podano:** $S \u003d 1 \\, \\text{cm}^2 \u003d 1 \\times 10^{-4} \\, \\text{m}^2$, $m_{pokrova} \u003d 0.2 \\, \\text{kg}$, $P_0 \u003d 1 \\, \\text{bar} \u003d 100 \\, \\text{kPa}$\n-   **Iščemo:** Višino vodnega stolpca ($h$), ki ga lahko zadrži pokrov.\n\n**Kontekst in analiza sil:**\nDa plošča ostane na mestu in ne pade dol, mora biti sila, ki pritiska nanjo od spodaj navzgor, enaka vsoti sil, ki pritiskata nanjo od zgoraj navzdol.\n-   **Sila navzgor:** To je sila zunanjega zračnega tlaka ($F_P$).\n-   **Sili navzdol:** To sta teža same plošče ($F_g$) in sila hidrostatičnega tlaka vodnega stolpca ($F_{tek}$).\n\n**Ravnovesje sil:**\n$$ F_P (\\uparrow) \u003d F_g (\\downarrow) + F_{tek} (\\downarrow) $$\n\n1.  **Razpis formul za sile:**\n    -   Sila zračnega tlaka: $F_P \u003d P_0 \\cdot S$\n    -   Sila teže: $F_g \u003d m \\cdot g$\n    -   Sila tekočine: $F_{tek} \u003d P_{tek} \\cdot S \u003d (\\rho \\cdot g \\cdot h) \\cdot S$\n2.  **Vstavimo v enačbo ravnovesja:**\n    $$ P_0 \\cdot S \u003d m \\cdot g + (\\rho \\cdot g \\cdot h) \\cdot S $$\n3.  **Izrazimo člen s neznanko h:**\n    $$ (\\rho \\cdot g \\cdot h) \\cdot S \u003d P_0 \\cdot S - m \\cdot g $$\n4.  **Izrazimo h:**\n    $$ h \u003d \\frac{P_0 \\cdot S - m \\cdot g}{\\rho \\cdot g \\cdot S} $$\n5.  **Vstavimo vrednosti in izračunamo:**\n    -   $P_0 \\cdot S \u003d 100000 \\, \\text{Pa} \\cdot 10^{-4} \\, \\text{m}^2 \u003d 10 \\, \\text{N}$\n    -   $m \\cdot g \u003d 0.2 \\, \\text{kg} \\cdot 9.81 \\, \\text{m/s}^2 \\approx 1.96 \\, \\text{N}$\n    -   $\\rho \\cdot g \\cdot S \u003d 1000 \\, \\frac{\\text{kg}}{\\text{m}^3} \\cdot 9.81 \\, \\frac{\\text{m}}{\\text{s}^2} \\cdot 10^{-4} \\, \\text{m}^2 \\approx 0.981 \\, \\frac{\\text{N}}{\\text{m}}$\n    $$ h \u003d \\frac{10 \\, \\text{N} - 1.96 \\, \\text{N}}{0.981 \\, \\frac{\\text{N}}{\\text{m}}} \u003d \\frac{8.04}{0.981} \\approx 8.2 \\, \\text{m} $$\n**Odgovor:** Pokrov lahko zadrži vodni stolpec z višino približno 8.2 metra.\n\n---\n\n#### Naloga 6b: Sprememba tlaka zraka\n\n-   **Podano:** Sprememba višine $\\Delta h \u003d 10 \\, \\text{m}$, Gostota zraka $\\rho_{zraka} \u003d 1.2 \\, \\text{kg/m}^3$\n-   **Iščemo:** Spremembo tlaka ($\\Delta P$).\n\n**Pojasnilo k vprašanju:**\nTo vprašanje naslavlja tvojo zmedenost. Tlak se spreminja z višino ne samo v vodi, ampak tudi v zraku, le da je učinek veliko manjši zaradi bistveno nižje gostote zraka.\n\n1.  **Formula:** Uporabimo isto formulo za hidrostatični tlak, le da vstavimo gostoto zraka.\n    $$ \\Delta P \u003d \\rho_{zraka} \\cdot g \\cdot \\Delta h $$\n2.  **Izračun:**\n    $$ \\Delta P \u003d 1.2 \\, \\frac{\\text{kg}}{\\text{m}^3} \\cdot 9.81 \\, \\frac{\\text{m}}{\\text{s}^2} \\cdot 10 \\, \\text{m} \\approx 118 \\, \\text{Pa} $$\n**Zaključek:** Če se povzpnemo za 10 metrov, se zračni tlak zmanjša za približno 118 Paskalov. To je zelo malo v primerjavi s celotnim zračnim tlakom, ki je okoli 100,000 Paskalov, zato ta učinek pri vsakdanjih problemih pogosto zanemarimo.\n\n---\n\n#### Naloga 8: Tlak ujetega zraka\n\n-   **Podano:** Višina posode $H \u003d 1 \\, \\text{m}$, Višina vodnega stolpca v cevi $h \u003d 20 \\, \\text{cm} \u003d 0.8 \\, \\text{m}$ od vrha posode, Zunanji tlak $P_0 \u003d 1 \\, \\text{bar}$\n-   **Iščemo:** Tlak ujetega zraka v cevi ($P$).\n\n**Analiza:**\nTlak na gladini vode **znotraj** obrnjene cevi je enak tlaku ujetega zraka ($P$). Tlak na **isti višini zunaj** cevi pa je enak vsoti zunanjega zračnega tlaka ($P_0$) in hidrostatičnega tlaka vode nad to točko. Ker sta tlaka na isti višini v mirujoči tekočini enaka, lahko zapišemo:\n$$ P \u003d P_0 + P_{hidrostatični} $$\n\n1.  **Izračun višinske razlike:**\n    Višina vodnega stolpca, ki povzroča dodaten tlak, je razlika med višino posode in višino vode v cevi: $H-h$.\n    -   $1 \\, \\text{m} - 0.2 \\, \\text{m} \u003d 0.8 \\, \\text{m}$\n2.  **Izračun tlaka:**\n    $$ P \u003d P_0 + \\rho_{vode} \\cdot g \\cdot (H-h) $$\n    $$ P \u003d 100000 \\, \\text{Pa} + (1000 \\, \\frac{\\text{kg}}{\\text{m}^3} \\cdot 10 \\, \\frac{\\text{m}}{\\text{s}^2} \\cdot 0.8 \\, \\text{m}) $$\n    $$ P \u003d 100000 \\, \\text{Pa} + 80000 \\, \\text{Pa} \u003d 180000 \\, \\text{Pa} $$\n    $$ P \u003d 1.8 \\, \\text{bar} $$\n\n*(Opomba: Izračun v zvezku je pravilen, le višina $h$ je bila narobe označena v skici. Pravilna višina za izračun tlaka je $0.8$ m, kot je bilo uporabljeno v formuli.)*\n\n---\n\n#### Naloga 10: Zlata zapestnica (Zlitina)\n\n-   **Podano:** Teža v zraku $F_{g,zrak} \u003d 0.49 \\, \\text{N}$, Teža v vodi $F_{g,voda} \u003d 0.44 \\, \\text{N}$, Gostota zlata $\\rho_{Au} \u003d 19.3 \\, \\text{g/cm}^3$, Gostota bakra $\\rho_{Cu} \u003d 8.9 \\, \\text{g/cm}^3$\n-   **Iščemo:** Delež zlata ($w(Au)$) in bakra ($w(Cu)$).\n\n**Strategija:** To je problem z dvema neznankama ($m_{Au}$ in $m_{Cu}$), zato potrebujemo dve enačbi.\n\n**1. Enačba: Skupna masa**\n-   Skupno maso dobimo iz teže v zraku:\n    $$ m_{skupaj} \u003d \\frac{F_{g,zrak}}{g} \u003d \\frac{0.49 \\, \\text{N}}{9.81 \\, \\text{m/s}^2} \\approx 0.05 \\, \\text{kg} \u003d 50 \\, \\text{g} $$\n-   Enačba 1:\n    $$ m_{Au} + m_{Cu} \u003d 50 \\, \\text{g} $$\n\n**2. Enačba: Skupni volumen**\n-   Skupni volumen dobimo iz sile vzgona. Vzgon je razlika med težo v zraku in težo v vodi.\n    $$ F_v \u003d F_{g,zrak} - F_{g,voda} \u003d 0.49 - 0.44 \u003d 0.05 \\, \\text{N} $$\n-   Vemo tudi, da je $F_v \u003d \\rho_{vode} \\cdot V_{skupaj} \\cdot g$.\n    $$ V_{skupaj} \u003d \\frac{F_v}{\\rho_{vode} \\cdot g} \u003d \\frac{0.05 \\, \\text{N}}{1000 \\, \\frac{\\text{kg}}{\\text{m}^3} \\cdot 10 \\, \\frac{\\text{m}}{\\text{s}^2}} \u003d 5 \\times 10^{-6} \\, \\text{m}^3 \u003d 5 \\, \\text{cm}^3 $$\n-   Enačba 2 (volumen je vsota volumnov komponent):\n    $$ V_{Au} + V_{Cu} \u003d 5 \\, \\text{cm}^3 $$\n    $$ \\frac{m_{Au}}{\\rho_{Au}} + \\frac{m_{Cu}}{\\rho_{Cu}} \u003d 5 \\, \\text{cm}^3 $$\n\n**3. Reševanje sistema enačb:**\n-   Iz enačbe 1 izrazimo $m_{Cu} \u003d 50 - m_{Au}$.\n-   Vstavimo v razširjeno enačbo 2:\n    $$ \\frac{m_{Au}}{19.3} + \\frac{50 - m_{Au}}{8.9} \u003d 5 $$\n-   To enačbo rešimo za $m_{Au}$ (pomnožimo z obema imenovalcema, uredimo...):\n    $$ 8.9 \\cdot m_{Au} + 19.3 \\cdot (50 - m_{Au}) \u003d 5 \\cdot 19.3 \\cdot 8.9 $$\n    $$ 8.9 m_{Au} + 965 - 19.3 m_{Au} \u003d 858.85 $$\n    $$ -10.4 m_{Au} \u003d -106.15 $$\n    $$ m_{Au} \\approx 10.2 \\, \\text{g} $$\n-   Izračunamo maso bakra:\n    $$ m_{Cu} \u003d 50 - 10.2 \u003d 39.8 \\, \\text{g} $$\n\n*(Opomba: Rezultati v zvezku (8.6 g in 41.4 g) se nekoliko razlikujejo. To je verjetno posledica zaokroževanja pri vmesnih korakih. Metodologija reševanja pa je pravilna.)*\n\n```",

I have re-checked your notebook and your screenshot of the Obsidian note, and your original calculation was the correct one. There was a simple arithmetic error in my analysis.\n\nThe hidrostatični pressure is indeed `8,000 Pa`, not `80,000 Pa`.\n\nHere is the corrected and clarified section for your `Fizika.md` file.\n\n```markdown\n#### Naloga 8: Tlak ujetega zraka\n\n-   **Podano:** Višina posode $H \u003d 1 \\, \\text{m}$, Višina vode v cevi je $h_{voda\\_v\\_cevi} \u003d 20 \\, \\text{cm} \u003d 0.2 \\, \\text{m}$ (merjeno od dna), Zunanji tlak $P_0 \u003d 1 \\, \\text{bar} \u003d 100 \\, \\text{kPa}$\n-   **Iščemo:** Tlak ujetega zraka v cevi ($P$).\n\n**Analiza:**\nTlak na gladini vode **zunaj** cevi ($P_1$) mora biti enak tlaku na isti višini **znotraj** cevi ($P_2$), ker je tekočina v ravnovesju.\n-   Tlak zunaj ($P_1$) je vsota zunanjega zračnega tlaka ($P_0$) in hidrostatičnega tlaka vodnega stolpca nad to točko.\n-   Tlak znotraj ($P_2$) je tlak ujetega zraka ($P$).\n\n**Enačba ravnovesja:**\n$$ P_{ujeti\\_zrak} \u003d P_0 + P_{hidrostatični} $$\n$$ P \u003d P_0 + \\rho_{vode} \\cdot g \\cdot (H - h_{voda\\_v\\_cevi}) $$\n\n**Izračun:**\nVišina vodnega stolpca, ki povzroča dodaten tlak, je $1 \\, \\text{m} - 0.2 \\, \\text{m} \u003d 0.8 \\, \\text{m}$.\n$$ P \u003d 100000 \\, \\text{Pa} + (1000 \\, \\frac{\\text{kg}}{\\text{m}^3} \\cdot 10 \\, \\frac{\\text{m}}{\\text{s}^2} \\cdot 0.8 \\, \\text{m}) $$\n$$ P \u003d 100000 \\, \\text{Pa} + 8000 \\, \\text{Pa} \u003d 108000 \\, \\text{Pa} $$\n$$ P \u003d 1.08 \\, \\text{bar} $$\n*(Opomba: Ta izračun je pravilen in se ujema z vašim zvezkom. Moja prejšnja analiza je vsebovala napako.)*\n```\n\n---\n\n### 2. Derivation: Why is $P \u003d \\rho g h$ valid?\n\nThis is a fantastic question. The formula for hydrostatic pressure is not arbitrary; it comes directly from the basic definition of pressure, $P \u003d F/S$.\n\nLet\u0027s derive it step-by-step. Imagine a column of water (like in a glass).\n\n1.  **Start with the basic definition of pressure.** The pressure at the bottom of the glass is the force exerted by the water divided by the area of the bottom.\n    $$ P \u003d \\frac{F}{S} $$\n\n2.  **What is the force?** The force the water exerts is simply its own weight ($F_g$).\n    $$ F \u003d F_g \u003d m \\cdot g $$\n    Where `m` is the mass of the water in the column.\n\n3.  **How do we get mass from density ($\\rho$)?** This is the key step. We know that density is mass per unit volume ($\\rho \u003d m/V$). We can rearrange this to express mass in terms of density and volume:\n    $$ m \u003d \\rho \\cdot V $$\n\n4.  **What is the volume of the water column?** The volume ($V$) of any regular column is its base area ($S$) multiplied by its height ($h$).\n    $$ V \u003d S \\cdot h $$\n\n5.  **Now, let\u0027s substitute everything back into the first equation:**\n    -   Start with: $P \u003d \\frac{F}{S}$\n    -   Substitute force with weight: $P \u003d \\frac{m \\cdot g}{S}$\n    -   Substitute mass with density and volume: $P \u003d \\frac{(\\rho \\cdot V) \\cdot g}{S}$\n    -   Substitute volume with area and height: $P \u003d \\frac{(\\rho \\cdot S \\cdot h) \\cdot g}{S}$\n\n6.  **Simplify.** The base area ($S$) appears in both the numerator and the denominator, so it cancels out!\n    $$ P \u003d \\rho \\cdot g \\cdot h $$\n\nThis is a crucial result. It shows that the pressure at a certain depth **does not depend on the shape of the container or the total amount of water**, but only on the **height** of the fluid column directly above it and the fluid\u0027s **density**.\n\n---\n\n### 3. \u0026 4. Sila Vzgona (Buoyancy Force) and Problem #9\n\nYou are right, the lecturer introduced the formula for buoyancy ($F_v$) as a tool to solve a problem. Let\u0027s give it a proper introduction first, and then the solution to problem #9 will make perfect sense.\n\n\u003e [!INFO] **Arhimedov zakon (Buoyancy)**\n\u003e\n\u003e **Načelo:** Telo, potopljeno v tekočino, je lažje za težo izpodrinjene tekočine. Ta \"izguba teže\" je posledica sile, imenovane **vzgon ($F_v$)**, ki deluje navpično navzgor.\n\u003e\n\u003e **Zakaj obstaja?** Ker tlak narašča z globino ($P \u003d \\rho g h$), je tlak na spodnji strani potopljenega telesa **večji** kot na zgornji strani. Ta razlika v tlakih povzroči neto silo navzgor.\n\u003e\n\u003e **Formula:**\n\u003e $$ F_v \u003d \\rho_{tek} \\cdot V_{pot} \\cdot g $$\n\u003e -   $\\rho_{tek}$ je gostota **tekočine** (npr. vode).\n\u003e -   $V_{pot}$ je volumen **potopljenega dela telesa** (torej volumen izpodrinjene tekočine).\n\n**Ravnovesje sil pri plavanju:**\nKo telo plava v mirovanju (kot lesena kocka v nalogi 9), to pomeni, da so sile v ravnovesju. Navpična sila navzgor (vzgon) je enaka navpični sili navzdol (teža).\n$$ F_v \u003d F_g $$\n\n**Rešitev problema #9 s to razlago:**\nSedaj, ko razumemo, zakaj je $F_v \u003d F_g$, postane rešitev jasna.\n\n1.  **Zapišemo ravnovesje sil:**\n    $$ F_v \u003d F_g $$\n\n2.  **Razširimo obe strani z definicijami:**\n    -   Sila vzgona ($F_v$) je teža **izpodrinjene VODE**: $F_v \u003d m_{vode} \\cdot g \u003d (\\rho_{vode} \\cdot V_{potopljen}) \\cdot g$\n    -   Sila teže ($F_g$) je teža **celotne KOCKE**: $F_g \u003d m_{kocke} \\cdot g \u003d (\\rho_{lesa} \\cdot V_{celoten}) \\cdot g$\n\n3.  **Vstavimo v enačbo ravnovesja:**\n    $$ (\\rho_{vode} \\cdot V_{potopljen}) \\cdot g \u003d (\\rho_{lesa} \\cdot V_{celoten}) \\cdot g $$\n\n4.  **Poenostavimo:** Težni pospešek $g$ se pokrajša na obeh straneh. To nam pove, da bi kocka plavala enako (isti delež pod vodo) na Zemlji ali na Luni!\n    $$ \\rho_{vode} \\cdot V_{potopljen} \u003d \\rho_{lesa} \\cdot V_{celoten} $$\n    Tukaj vidite, kako je **razmerje gostot** neposredno povezano z **razmerjem volumnov**. To je povezava, ki ti je manjkala.\n\n5.  **Izrazimo volumne s stranico kocke (a) in potopljeno višino (x):**\n    -   $V_{potopljen} \u003d a \\cdot a \\cdot x \u003d a^2 \\cdot x$\n    -   $V_{celoten} \u003d a \\cdot a \\cdot a \u003d a^3$\n    $$ \\rho_{vode} \\cdot (a^2 \\cdot x) \u003d \\rho_{lesa} \\cdot a^3 $$\n\n6.  **Rešimo za x:** Vidimo, da se $a^2$ pokrajša.\n    $$ x \u003d a \\cdot \\frac{\\rho_{lesa}}{\\rho_{vode}} $$\n    Ta elegantna formula nam pove, da je delež potopljene višine enak razmerju med gostoto telesa in gostoto tekočine.\n\n7.  **Končni izračun:**\n    $$ x \u003d 20 \\, \\text{cm} \\cdot \\frac{0.8 \\, \\text{g/cm}^3}{1.0 \\, \\text{g/cm}^3} \u003d 16 \\, \\text{cm} $$",

      "text": "You are asking the perfect question. This is one of the biggest challenges in physics education: learning to translate a simplified, abstract sketch into a real-world physical system. Your feeling of being \"in the dark\" is completely normal because it requires recognizing a standard pattern.\n\nLet\u0027s break this down completely. The key is that the diagram is not a literal picture of a single machine, but a **schematic representing two simple machines working together**:\n\n1.  A **Lever** (Vzvod)\n2.  A **Hydraulic Press** (Hidravlično dvigalo)\n\n### Justification: Recognizing the \"Two Machines in One\"\n\nThink of it as a system where the output of the first machine becomes the input for the second.\n\n**Machine 1: The Lever (The 15cm and 3cm part)**\n\nYou asked: \"How does one know that there is a stopper between the 15cm line and the 3cm line?\"\n\nThat \"stopper,\" represented by the small triangle (`△`), is the **fulcrum** (vrtišče ali opora). This is the universally recognized symbol for a pivot point in a lever system.\n\nWhen you see a diagram like this:\n\n```\nSila navzdol ↓\n             |\n             ------------------O------------------- Bat (Piston)\n                           △\n                         Vrtišče\n```\n\n...you are trained to immediately recognize it as a **lever**.\n\n*   **The 15 cm part** is the **effort arm** (ročica sile). This is where you apply your force (`F_vzvoda`).\n*   **The 3 cm part** is the **resistance arm** (ročica bremena). This is where the lever does its work.\n*   **The purpose of this lever** is to provide **mechanical advantage**. You press down with a small force (20 N) over a long distance (15 cm) to produce a larger force (100 N) over a short distance (3 cm).\n\n**Machine 2: The Hydraulic Press (The part with S₁ and S₂)**\n\nThis is the system with the two pistons and the fluid, which works on **Pascal\u0027s Principle**.\n\n*   **The input** to this machine is the force `F₁` (100 N) pressing down on the small piston `S₁`.\n*   **The output** is the much larger force `F₂` (500 N) being exerted by the large piston `S₂`.\n\n### Connecting the Two Machines\n\nNow, let\u0027s put it together. The brilliance of this system is how they are linked:\n\n\u003e The **output force of the lever** (`F₁`) is the **input force for the hydraulic press**.\n\nHere is a clearer, annotated interpretation of the sketch from your notes:\n\n```\n  (Ti pritisneš tukaj)\n       F_vzvoda ↓\n                |\n                | \u003c---- Ročica sile (15 cm) ----\u003e |\n                |                                 |\n  --------------O---------------------------------△--------------------\n                | \u003c--- Ročica bremena (3 cm) ---\u003e |\n                |                               Vrtišče (tvoj \"stopper\")\n                ↓ F₁ (Sila, ki jo vzvod pritisne na bat)\n             -------\n            | Bat S₁|\n  +---------+       +---------+\n  |         |\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d|         |\n  | Tekočina|\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d\u003d| Tekočina|\n  |         +-------+         | ↑ F₂ (Sila, ki dvigne breme)\n  |                           |\n  +---------------------------+\n                             | Bat S₂|\n                             -------\n```\n\n### The Justification: Why This Interpretation is Correct\n\n1.  **Standard Physics Schematics:** The drawing of a line on a triangle pivot is the standard, textbook way to represent a lever. It\u0027s a visual shorthand that physicists and engineers use. The skill you\u0027re learning here is to recognize these fundamental patterns.\n2.  **Logical Flow of Forces:** The problem asks you to find the force `F₁` needed for the press, and then asks you to find the force on the handle. This implies a two-step process. First, you figure out the press (`F₁ \u003d 100 N`). Then you ask, \"What kind of simple machine could I use to generate that 100 N force with even less effort?\" A lever is the perfect tool for that. The problem combines these two concepts.\n3.  **Mechanical Advantage:** The entire point of such a machine is to make work easier. You apply a tiny force of **20 N** and end up lifting something that requires **500 N**. The system achieves this in two stages:\n    *   **Lever:** Amplifies your 20 N force by 5 times (15 cm / 3 cm) to get 100 N.\n    *   **Hydraulic Press:** Amplifies the 100 N force by another 5 times (5 cm² / 1 cm²) to get the final 500 N.\n\n**So, you were not in the dark.** You correctly identified that the diagram was unusual. The key insight is that it\u0027s not a drawing of one object, but a schematic of two principles working in sequence. Your job as a student is to learn to spot these principles (levers, hydraulics, inclined planes, etc.) even when they are represented by simple lines and shapes.",

      "text": "That is the single most important and perhaps most counter-intuitive concept in hydraulics, and you are absolutely right to be baffled by it. Once you understand this, the whole topic clicks into place.\n\nThe short answer is **Pascal\u0027s Principle**.\n\nThe longer, more intuitive answer involves thinking about what pressure *is* and how a liquid behaves when it\u0027s trapped.\n\nLet\u0027s use a simple analogy.\n\n### The Toothpaste Analogy\n\nImagine you have a full tube of toothpaste with the cap on. The toothpaste is your **incompressible fluid**, and the tube is your **confined container**.\n\n\n\n1.  **You Squeeze One End (Input):** You use your thumb to apply a force on a small area at the bottom of the tube. This creates **pressure** at that spot.\n    `Pressure \u003d Force / Area`\n\n2.  **What Happens Inside?** The toothpaste can\u0027t be compressed or squashed into a smaller volume. It has to go somewhere. The \"push\" you applied with your thumb is instantly transmitted by the molecules of the paste to **every single point inside that tube**. The pressure doesn\u0027t just travel in a straight line; it pushes outwards in all directions equally. It pushes on the left side of the tube, the right side, the top, the bottom, and, crucially, it pushes on the cap.\n\n3.  **The Result (Output):** If you take the cap off, that transmitted pressure acts on the area of the opening, and the force it creates pushes the toothpaste out.\n\nThe key takeaway is that the pressure you created at the bottom is felt **identically** at the opening.\n\n---\n\n### Applying this to the Hydraulic Press\n\nNow, let\u0027s replace the toothpaste tube with our hydraulic system.\n\n-   The **incompressible fluid** is the oil or water inside.\n-   The **confined container** is the sealed tubing.\n-   Your **thumb** is the small piston ($S_1$).\n-   The **opening** is the large piston ($S_2$).\n\nHere’s the step-by-step logic of **Pascal\u0027s Principle**:\n\n**Step 1: Pressure is Created**\nWhen you push down on the small piston ($S_1$) with a force ($F_1$), you are creating a pressure in the fluid directly beneath it.\n$$ P_1 \u003d \\frac{F_1}{S_1} $$\n\n**Step 2: Pressure is Transmitted Undiminished**\nBecause the fluid is **confined** and **incompressible**, this pressure increase is transmitted instantly and equally to **every point within the fluid**. The molecules of the fluid act like tiny, tightly packed billiard balls – a push on one is immediately passed on to its neighbors, and so on, until the push is felt everywhere.\n\nThis means the pressure at the bottom of the large piston ($S_2$) is **exactly the same** as the pressure you created at the small piston.\n$$ P_1 \u003d P_2 $$\nThis is the heart of the matter. **The pressure is the same everywhere in the fluid.**\n\n**Step 3: A New Force is Created**\nNow, this transmitted pressure ($P_2$) acts on the large area of the second piston ($S_2$). The force it creates is:\n$$ F_2 \u003d P_2 \\cdot S_2 $$\n\n### The \"Magic\" of Force Multiplication\n\nThis is where the magic happens. Since we know $P_1 \u003d P_2$, we can substitute:\n$$ \\frac{F_1}{S_1} \u003d \\frac{F_2}{S_2} $$\n\nLet\u0027s use the numbers from your problem:\n-   Area $S_1 \u003d 1 \\, \\text{cm}^2$\n-   Area $S_2 \u003d 5 \\, \\text{cm}^2$\n\nYou apply a force $F_1 \u003d 100 \\, \\text{N}$ to the small piston. This creates a pressure:\n$$ P_1 \u003d \\frac{100 \\, \\text{N}}{1 \\, \\text{cm}^2} \u003d 100 \\, \\frac{\\text{N}}{\\text{cm}^2} $$\n\nThat pressure of $100 \\, \\text{N/cm}^2$ is transmitted everywhere. It now pushes up on the large piston. The upward force on that piston is:\n$$ F_2 \u003d P_2 \\cdot S_2 \u003d \\left(100 \\, \\frac{\\text{N}}{\\text{cm}^2}\\right) \\cdot (5 \\, \\text{cm}^2) \u003d 500 \\, \\text{N} $$\n\n**Conclusion and Justification:**\n\nYou can justify the interpretation because of **Pascal\u0027s Principle**, which states that pressure in a confined, incompressible fluid is transmitted equally in all directions.\n\n-   **Why is the pressure the same?** Because the fluid molecules are pushing on each other equally everywhere.\n-   **Why is the force different?** Because that **same pressure** is acting on two **different areas**. A small force on a small area creates a pressure that can generate a huge force on a large area.",

      "text": "That is an absolutely brilliant and perfectly logical question. Your intuition is picking up on a fundamental concept, but it\u0027s applying it to the wrong quantity. You are thinking about the force being \"spread out,\" but in hydraulics, the force isn\u0027t the thing that stays the same.\n\n**The PRESSURE is the thing that stays the same.**\n\nLet\u0027s use a different analogy to make this crystal clear: **Thumb Tacks.**\n\n### Scenario 1: One Thumb Tack (Small Area)\n\nImagine you push your thumb against the sharp point of a single thumb tack with a gentle force.\n-   **Force (F):** Small\n-   **Area (S):** Tiny (the point of the tack)\n-   **Pressure (P \u003d F/S):** **HUGE!** It hurts because the entire force is concentrated on a tiny area, creating immense pressure.\n\n### Scenario 2: A Bed of Nails (Large Area)\n\nNow, imagine lying on a bed of hundreds of nails. Your entire body weight is the force.\n-   **Force (F):** Large (your whole weight)\n-   **Area (S):** Huge (the combined area of all the nail points)\n-   **Pressure (P \u003d F/S):** **TINY!** You can lie on it safely because your weight is distributed over a huge area, so the pressure at any single point is too small to break the skin.\n\n### The Key Insight: Flipping the Logic\n\nYour question comes from the \"Bed of Nails\" logic: a force spread over a large area results in low pressure.\n\nBut a hydraulic press works in the **exact opposite way**. It uses the \"Thumb Tack\" logic in reverse.\n\nHere\u0027s how to think about it correctly:\n\n1.  **You create a pressure.** By pushing the small piston (`S₁`) with a small force (`F₁`), you are creating a pressure inside the fluid. Because the area `S₁` is small, you can create a significant pressure without a huge amount of force (like the thumb tack).\n    $$ P \u003d \\frac{F_1}{S_1} $$\n\n2.  **This pressure is transmitted everywhere.** Pascal\u0027s Principle tells us this pressure, `P`, is now present at every single point inside the fluid, pushing in all directions.\n\n3.  **The pressure acts on the large piston.** Now, think of the large piston (`S₂`) as the *reverse* of the bed of nails. You have a pressure `P` that is pushing on **every single square centimeter** of that large piston.\n    -   Pressure `P` pushes on the first cm²...\n    -   Pressure `P` pushes on the second cm²...\n    -   Pressure `P` pushes on the third cm²...\n    -   ...and so on for the entire area `S₂`.\n\n4.  **The forces add up.** The total upward force on the large piston (`F₂`) is the sum of all these small forces. It\u0027s the pressure `P` multiplied by the total area `S₂`.\n    $$ F_2 \u003d P \\cdot S_2 $$\n\nBecause `S₂` is much larger than `S₁`, the resulting force `F₂` will be much larger than the initial force `F₁`.\n\n### The \"No Free Lunch\" Principle: The Trade-Off\n\nSo, if we can multiply force so easily, are we getting something for nothing? No. The universe always has a trade-off. In this case, we are trading **distance** for **force**.\n\nThe amount of **work** done on both sides must be the same (Work \u003d Force × Distance).\n$$ W_1 \u003d W_2 $$\n$$ F_1 \\cdot d_1 \u003d F_2 \\cdot d_2 $$\n\nSince the output force `F₂` is much bigger than the input force `F₁`, the input distance `d₁` must be much bigger than the output distance `d₂`.\n\n**In simple terms:** To lift the massive car on the large piston by just **1 centimeter**, you have to push the small piston down by **5 centimeters**. You have to push for a longer distance to get the benefit of the larger force.\n\n---\n\n### Final Summary\n\n| Quantity | What Happens? | Analogy |\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\n| **Pressure (P)** | **STAYS THE SAME** everywhere in the fluid. | The \"push\" from the toothpaste is felt equally everywhere. |\n| **Force (F)** | Is **MULTIPLIED** on the larger piston. | The pressure acts on every square cm of the large area, and these forces add up. |\n| **Distance (d)** | Is **REDUCED** on the larger piston. | You have to push the small piston much further to move the large piston a little. |\n\nSo, to answer your question directly: The force isn\u0027t \"spread out\" and weakened. Instead, a **constant pressure** acts over a larger area, and the forces on each part of that area **add up** to create a much larger total force.",
